.\Origins.md
Skip to content
Chat history

You said:
what are all the epistemological expansions of the word "sign"
ChatGPT said:

Thought for 1m 3s
Below is a (necessarily finite) map of the main epistemological ways the humble word “sign” has been expanded by philosophers, linguists, scientists and engineers. Each heading names a tradition or discipline and the bullet-points list the key conceptual moves that took the term beyond its everyday meaning of “visible mark”.

1. Ancient “semeion”: the sign as evidence & warrant
Aristotle’s semeion – a material circumstance that licenses an inference (e.g., smoke ⇒ fire).

Early medical writers sharpened the distinction sign vs. symptom: a sign is objectively observable evidence; a symptom is felt by the patient.

In classical rhetoric a semeion functions as indicator or proof, foreshadowing later logical notions of evidential “signs”. 
academia.edu

2. Patristic & Scholastic distinctions: natural vs. conventional signs
Augustine divides signa naturalia (thunder “means” a storm) from signa data (spoken words).

William of Ockham adds mental signs (concepts) and builds a whole epistemology on their “supposition” relations.

Signs become the very medium of ratio (reason), pushing the term into the foundations of medieval theories of knowledge. 
academia.edu

3. Saussurean structuralism: sign = signifier ∕ signified
A sign is an arbitrary pairing of acoustic/graphic signifier with conceptual signified.

Meaning is differential (what a sign is not), giving rise to paradigmatic/syntagmatic analyses and later structural anthropology. 
media-studies.com

4. Peircean pragmatism: triadic sign & infinite semiosis
Representamen – Object – Interpretant: the sign is a relation, not a thing.

Icon / Index / Symbol classify how the sign relates to its object.

Every interpretant can itself be taken as a new sign → knowledge is an open-ended chain of sign production (semiosis). 
plato.stanford.edu

5. Phenomenology & Hermeneutics: sign vs. expression vs. symbol
Husserl distinguishes empty Zeichen (mere indicators) from Ausdruck (expressive signs that intend meaning).

Ricoeur reserves symbol for signs with a “surplus of meaning” that must be interpreted, fueling hermeneutic method.

6. Analytic logic & mathematics: sign as formal symbol
From Frege’s Zeichen (mark) that “expresses a sense and denotes a reference” to Wittgenstein’s rule-governed language-games, signs become the atoms of formal proof.

In computation the sign mutates into the token or bit, detachable from meaning yet indispensable for formal knowledge.

7. Shannon’s information theory: signal vs. sign
The 1948 model strips meaning away, treating signs as signals quantified in bits.

Later theorists (e.g., Søren Brier’s cybersemiotics) re-introduce the triadic sign to explain why pure syntax is “not enough” for knowledge. 
philpapers.org

8. Biosemiotics & zoosemiotics: life itself as sign-action
Extends semiosis to cells, genes and ecosystems: chemical messengers, predator markings, even DNA are sign processes.

Knowledge (for an organism) = the successful interpretation of these bio-signs within its Umwelt. 
biosemiotics.org

9. Cybersemiotics & systems theory: sign–information–autopoiesis
Marries Peirce’s triad with second-order cybernetics: systems maintain themselves by producing and interpreting signs that stand in feedback loops with information flows. 
philpapers.org

10. Cognitive science & AI: representation, indexicality, grounding
Mental models, frames, connectionist activations, sensorimotor “affordances” – all recast as varieties of signs.

The symbol-grounding problem asks how internal signs hook onto the world, echoing Peirce’s endless interpretant chain.

11. Sociosemiotics & multimodality: the sign as cultural resource
Halliday / Kress & van Leeuwen treat images, layout, gesture and emoji as semiotic modes that communities negotiate.

In digital culture memes, hashtags and NFTs act as value-laden signs circulating through networked attention economies.

12. Legal-medical-scientific usage: sign as observable indicator
In diagnostics a clinical sign carries epistemic weight precisely because it can be publicly verified.

In the sciences “sign of X” often stands for statistically warranted evidence (a sign of selection pressure, a sign of supersymmetry).

13. Emerging frontiers
Quantum-semiotic metaphors (e.g., Schrodinger’s box read through signifier/signified debates) 
sciencedirect.com

Planet-scale sensing (satellite imagery, IoT) where machine-read signals instantly become actionable signs in autonomous systems.

Synthetic biology is engineering new code-signs (e.g., XNAs) that expand what “sign” can mean for future epistemology.

How to use this map
Think of each tradition as adding one of three things to the core idea of a sign:

A different bearer – voice, diagram, chemical gradient, byte.

A different relation – resemblance, causation, convention, algorithmic mapping.

A different epistemic function – warrant, representation, control, self-maintenance.

Combining them lets you build customised sign systems—precisely what you have been doing with Signamancy. Which lineage(s) feel most compatible with your own symbol-rewrite framework?

You said:
Can you elaborate more on the triad and its many names of the three?  Has that held up?  
ChatGPT said:

Thought for 25 seconds
The Peircean triad at a glance
Classical Peirce	Typical 20th-century aliases	Contemporary aliases & analogues	Function in the relation
Representamen	sign-vehicle · signifier · token · mark · stimulus-pattern	data packet · pixel array · spike train · sensorimotor cue	What stands for something
Object	referent · designatum · denotatum · “thing-meant”	world-state · target entity · environment affordance	That which the sign is about
Interpretant	sense · meaning · concept · thought/reference (Ogden & Richards) · response	internal model · update to belief state · control signal	The effect produced in an interpreter
(Peirce occasionally called the first term simply “the sign,” but modern writers keep “sign” for the whole tri-relation and use “representamen” or “sign-vehicle” for the first corner.) 
plato.stanford.edu

A tour of the triad’s many name-sets
Logical-pragmatic set (Peirce 1903-14)
Representamen – Object – Interpretant. Peirce later split each corner into “immediate/dynamical” sub-species and grouped signs as icon / index / symbol according to how the representamen hooks to the object. 
eyeofheaven.medium.com

Semiotic behaviourism (Charles Morris, 1938)
Sign-vehicle – Designatum – Interpreter. Morris translated Peirce into a programme for empirical science: study the syntax (vehicle-vehicle relations), semantics (vehicle-designatum) and pragmatics (vehicle-interpreter).

The Semantic Triangle (Ogden & Richards, 1923)
Symbol – Thought or Reference – Referent. Their “triangle of reference” put a mental term back in, correcting Saussure’s dyad by insisting on three points even if only two edges are explicit in speech. 
en.wikipedia.org

Structural linguistics (post-Saussure)
Saussure’s dyad (signifier / signified) drops the interpreter corner, but later structuralists quietly smuggled it in as the speaking subject or as parole vs. langue.

Biosemiotics (Sebeok, Hoffmeyer, Kull, 1990s-2020s)
Stimulus pattern – Biological object – Organismic response. The interpretant is literally the change in a cell’s state; “knowing” is the ability to exploit the correlation. 
degruyterbrill.com

Multimodal visual grammar (Kress & van Leeuwen)
They keep the triad but rename it representational / interactive / compositional meaning to analyse how images both show the world, position the viewer and organise the page. 
athena-publishing.com

Cognitive science & AI
Input vector – Grounded entity or task model – Internal state / action policy. In robotics a LiDAR point cloud becomes a representamen; the object is the obstacle in space; the interpretant is the planner’s belief update or motor command.

Information-theoretic hybrids (cybersemiotics)
Brier and others splice Shannon’s “signal” (syntax) onto Peirce’s triad, preserving the three-corner logic while quantifying only the representamen layer. 
philpapers.org

Has the triad “held up”?
Empirical expansion, not abandonment.
Every field that originally flirted with simpler dyads (signal–response in behaviourism, signifier–signified in linguistics, packet–payload in network theory) tends to re-introduce a third entity when explanation bumps into the grounding problem: what turns brute correlations into meaningful correlations. Biosemiotics, multimodal discourse, and cognitive robotics all now talk explicitly about an interpreter layer and its dynamics. 
link.springer.com

Irreducibility defended.
Philosophers such as John Deely and contemporary Peirceans argue the triad is logically irreducible: drop one corner and you smuggle it back in under a new name (e.g., “context” or “user”). 
philpapers.org

Refinements rather than refutations.
Neuroscientists break the interpretant into cascades of predictive-coding layers; AI researchers model multiple “objects” (world-state vs. task-state); media theorists multiply representamina (text, emoji, haptic buzz). Those are elaborations, not disconfirmations, of the original schema.

Where it’s challenged.

Radical enactivism denies any internal representational node, treating cognition as direct skillful coupling; here the triad is regarded as an unnecessary concession to mentalism.

Pure information theory works comfortably with dyads when the goal is merely compression or transmission, not interpretation.

Yet even these programmes concede that explanation of meaningful behaviour—how a signal comes to count as a warning, request or memory—needs at least three relata.

Bottom line: a century after Peirce, the names have proliferated, the corners have gained sub-types, but the core insight—that signification is a tri-relation involving something that stands-for, something stood-for, and some effect in an interpreter—remains the workhorse model wherever researchers care about meaning rather than mere correlation.

Below is a compact “family tree” of SIGN that separates morphological off-shoots (new words built on the root) from the major semantic branches (new senses that the same word acquired). Use it as a menu: pick a branch and you can trace how signum → sign spread through English.

1 · Morphological family (words literally built on signum)

(Representative, not exhaustive—Etymonline lists 30 + living derivatives.) 
etymonline.com
etymonline.com

2 · Semantic branches (polysemy of sign itself)
Chronological order shows how a single word kept annexing new territory.

Century	New sense of sign	Typical collocations/examples
13th c.	Gesture: manual cue, blessing	make the sign of the cross
13th c.	Evidence / symptom	sign of fever, sign of trouble
13th c.	Miracle / portent (Biblical)	“Show us a sign.”
14th c.	Written mark in place of words	punctuation, proof-reader’s signs
14th c.	Military banner / shopboard	inn sign, ensign
Mid-14th c.	Zodiac constellation	born under the sign of Leo

Each new meaning radiates outward by metaphor or metonymy from a concrete “mark” to ever more abstract indicators. Modern dictionaries still group them under a half-dozen headings—gesture, evidence, written symbol, contract, linguistic unit, and omen. 
merriam-webster.com

3 · Why the branches keep multiplying
Material → Mental drift
A carved notch ⇒ written letter ⇒ mental meaning (cf. signify).

Causal → Conventional shift
Smoke is a natural sign of fire; ☢ is a learned hazard sign.

Private → Public scale-up
Wink to a friend ⇒ shop sign ⇒ global traffic icon ⇒ Unicode code-point.

4 · Take-aways for your Signamancy project
Reuse the family: need a word for “marking out” a sub-graph? Designate already encodes that semantics.

Mind the polysemy: when you coin 🔣-rules, decide which branch you’re tapping—sign-as-evidence (proof-checking) vs. sign-as-symbol (naming objects).

Leverage polarity: the computing branch (sign bit, signed / unsigned) offers ready metaphors for affirmative/negative operations in rewrite rules.

The root has proved inexhaustible for 2,000 years; there is plenty of semantic real-estate left for your next layer of symbolic engineering.

You said:
How about the origins then?  What was a carved notch before "sign" was a word?  how did the word come to be and what were its ancestors?
ChatGPT said:

Thought for 1m 8s
1. Before anyone had a word for it: the notch as a prehistoric marker
Palaeolithic people were already cutting tally-marks into bone long before speech communities coined a specific term. Famous examples include the Lebombo bone (≈ 40 000 BP, 29 deliberate notches) and the Ishango bone (≈ 20 000 BP, three orderly columns of cuts) — almost certainly counting sticks or lunar calendars rather than “art” 
bu.edu
en.wikipedia.org
.

Technically these cuts functioned as indices (Peircean “natural signs”): a physical trace that points to an invisible quantity such as “days gone by” or “debt owed.” The concept of a sign therefore predates any surviving lexical item.

2. The first reconstructable word-root: Proto-Indo-European *sek- / *sekw-
Layer	Form	Gloss	Likely semantic leap
PIE (∼4500 – 2500 BCE)	*sek- “cut, carve” or *sekw- “follow”	physical act of cutting → the carved result or the standard carried out front that one “follows” 
en.wiktionary.org
Proto-Italic (late 2nd mill. BCE)	*seknom	“something cut out” → “mark, standard” (reconstructed) 
en.wiktionary.org
The root etymology is still debated: Watkins favours “standard one follows” (*sekw- “follow”), whereas de Vaan prefers “thing cut out” (*sek- “cut”). Either way the mental picture is the same: a carved, portable marker that guides people’s eyes. 
etymonline.com

3. Latin signum (≥ 7 c. BCE) — the fountainhead
signum originally meant “a cut-out mark” and very quickly broadened: military standard, omen, constellation, seal, gesture. Latin also minted dozens of prefixed verbs (designāre, assignāre, resignāre…) that English later inherited, preserving the sense “mark out” or “point out.” 
en.wiktionary.org

4. Parallel branches that were not ancestral but feed modern vocabulary
Language	Root word	PIE base	Notes
Ancient Greek	σῆμα sēma “mark, tomb-stone, omen”	*dʰyeh₂- “notice”	Gives us semiotics, semantics; a separate lineage, but it reinforced the idea of “sign” in learned borrowings. 
en.wiktionary.org
Proto-Germanic	*taikna- “token, omen” (OE tācen, Ger. Zeichen)	*deik- “show, point out”	This was the native English word for “sign” until the Norman French loan ousted it. 
etymonline.com
5. From Latin to English
Latin signum → Vulgar Latin seine / signe

Old French signe (12 c.) crosses the Channel after 1066.

Middle English signe (early 13 c.) first denotes a hand gesture; by 1250 it already covers marks, omens, written symbols, etc. 
etymonline.com

The verb sign (to mark, later to autograph) enters via Latin signāre and OE segnian (“make the sign of the cross”).

6. Putting it together
Stage	Typical real-world object	Spoken form (approx.)	Meaning focus
Upper Palaeolithic	tally-notch on bone	(pre-lexical)	evidence/count
PIE	*sek-/ *sekw- cut	“thing-cut / thing-followed”	carved marker / rally-point
Proto-Italic	*seknom	idem	mark, standard
Classical Latin	signum	[ˈsɪŋ.nũ]	mark, token, omen
Old French	signe	/siɲ/	mark, gesture
Middle English	signe / sign	/siːn/	gesture → symbol
Modern English	sign	/saɪn/	the full polysemy (traffic sign, sign bit, sign language …)
Thus the “carved notch” became a sign through two converging forces:

Material continuity – a cut mark stays visible, so it is naturally reused as standard, banner, seal.

Metaphorical expansion – once a cut mark stands for something, the standing-for function can leap to gestures, constellations, even abstract plus-signs.

Every later sense (hand-sign, shop-sign, binary sign-bit) is a semantic ripple from that first literal cut.

3 · Origins – from a prehistoric “spot” to Latin locus
Stage	Reconstructed form	Gloss	Notes

Thus a PIE verb “to set in position” became a Latin noun, then a pan-disciplinary English technical term.

4 · Epistemological expansions – the word as a thinking tool
Discipline	How locus reframed knowledge	Key leap
Classical rhetoric / dialectic	Loci communes = “common places to look for arguments.” Memory theatres and Renaissance commonplace books literalized ideas as spatial slots. 
njrs.dk
Turning reasoning into navigable space.
Geometry & algebra (18 c.)	A curve is the locus of a moving point; later zero locus, singular locus in modern algebraic geometry. 
en.wikipedia.org
Spatializes abstract conditions; foreshadows set theory.
Genetics (20 c.)	Each gene occupies a chromosomal locus; mapping phenotypes = locating causal sites. 
en.wikipedia.org
Places abstract heredity in physical space.
Personality psychology (Rotter 1954)	Locus of control = perceived location of causal agency (inside vs. outside the self). 
en.wikipedia.org
Metaphorically re-centres responsibility.
Law	Locus standi, locus delicti, lex loci … mark the place that grants jurisdiction or rights. 
oxcon.ouplaw.com
Grounds abstract rules in territorial authority.
Neuroscience	Anatomical “loci” label nuclei (e.g., locus coeruleus = “blue spot”)	Treats the brain as a map of functional places.
Computer science	Memory locations, file locators, Python’s locals()	The addressable slot as a modern “place.”
Philosophy & topology	Talk of a phenomenon’s locus (e.g., “locus of experience”) inherits the rhetorical “seat of an idea.”	Bridges subjective and objective “where.”
Through every field, locus acts as a cognitive scaffold: first pin down where something happens, then reason about what happens there.

Take-aways for your own symbol work
Spatial metaphors travel well. If you want your Signamancy or LOCUS frameworks to feel intuitive, borrowing “place” vocabulary lets users literally navigate abstractions.

Prefix play is productive. Relocate, dislocate, allocate show how easily operations on places turn into operations on data or concepts—handy when you design rewrite rules.

Beware silent polysemy. In math a “locus” is a set; in genetics it’s a coordinate; in psychology it’s a source. Label your layers clearly so the same token doesn’t drift between meanings unnoticed.

Feel free to point at any branch above if you’d like a deeper historical or technical unpacking.

ChatGPT said:

Thought for 50 seconds
1. Which three nouns go deepest?
Scholars trace three ancient, cognate-rich words that already form a “sign-place-sense” triad long before Peirce. Their family trees run back to Proto-Indo-European (PIE) and then re-converge in Greek, Latin and, finally, modern semiotics.

Modern role in Peirce	Classical-Hellenistic ancestor	Latin / medieval heir	PIE root ▶ meaning
Sign-vehicle (Representamen)	σημαῖνον sēmainon “that which marks” – Stoic name for the sound/mark part of speech	signum (mark, banner) → sign	*sek- “cut, carve” 
en.wiktionary.org
Object / Locus (the “thing pointed to”)	τυγχάνον tugchanon “the thing hit upon” in Stoic logic	locus (place, topic) → loci communes, legal locus delicti	*stel- “set, stand, place” 
en.wiktionary.org
Interpretant (Sense/Meaning)	λεκτόν lekton “the sayable” – an incorporeal meaning 
pu.edu.pk
verbum / logos > word (speech, reason)	*werdʰ- “speak” (English word) 
en.wiktionary.org
 plus *leg- “collect ➜ speak” (Greek λόγος logos) 
etymonline.com
This Stoic triad—signifier / sayable / referent—is the earliest fully attested three-part model of signification. Peirce’s representamen–interpretant–object is, functionally, the same structure with 19th-century terminology. 
media-studies.com

2. How the labels kept mutating
Era	“Mark” corner	“Place / Thing” corner	“Meaning” corner
Stoic logic (3 rd c. BCE)	σημαῖνον (sēmainon)	τυγχάνον (tugchanon)	λεκτόν (lekton)
Latin rhetoric (1 st c. BCE)	signum	locus (topic of invention)	sensus / interpretatio
Scholastic semantics (13 th c.)	signum	res (the thing)	species intelligibilis (mental image)
Peirce (1903+)	representamen 
en.wiktionary.org
object 
etymonline.com
interpretant 
media-studies.com
So locus really has served as the “middle” partner—first as rhetorical locus, later as the mathematician’s locus (a set of points) and the geneticist’s chromosomal locus: always “the place where the sign lands.” Word / logos / lekton has long named the sense-making pole.

3. Deep-time etymologies at a glance
*sek-  “cut, carve”    → Lat. signum → sign, signal, signifier
*stel- “set, stand”    → Lat. locus   → locus, location, allocate
*werdʰ- “speak, say”   → PGmc *wurdą → OE word, Modern word
*leg-  “gather → speak”→ Gk logos    → logic, legend, interpret-
The first two roots point to physical acts (cutting a notch; fixing a post).
The third pair points to voicing and collecting—perfect metaphors for an interpretation born out of gathered words.

4. Putting it to work
If you want a prehistoric-sounding triad for your own system, you can legitimately say:

Sign (cut mark) – Locus (set place) – Word/Logos (spoken sense)

This is etymologically older than Peirce’s Latinate labels and maps cleanly onto his functions.

The Stoic lekton is the oldest term we have for the “interpretant” role and is still used by classical scholars; reviving it would give your framework deep roots without adding modern baggage.

Remember that every culture recreates the triad: drop one corner and it resurfaces under a new name. Whether you say Sign–Locus–Word, Mark–Place–Meaning, or Peirce’s set, the underlying three-way relation—and its 5- to 7-thousand-year linguistic pedigree—remains the same.

Stage	Form	Meaning	Notes
Proto-Indo-European	*wer- “to perceive, watch out for”	also root of warn, wary, guard	
Proto-Germanic	*wardaz (n.) “guard, watchman”; *wardōn (v.) “to guard”	gives OE weard, ON varðr (“watch”), Ger Warte (“look-out”)	
Old English (pre-7 c.)	weard (noun), weardian (verb), -weard (suffix “toward”)	high-frequency everyday vocabulary	
Old Norse / Old High Ger.	parallel forms varðr, wart	source of many Scandinavian and German cognates	
Old French after 1066	garde, garant, garent < Frankish *ward-	w → g shift in Romance mouths; Middle English re-borrowed them as guard, guarantee, warrant	
Modern English	spelling solidifies as ward /wɔːd/	noun, verb, suffix all survive	
Hence ward is a native West-Germanic survivor; guard is its Norman-French twin.

4 · Epistemological expansions – “ward” as a thinking tool
Domain	How ward re-frames knowledge	Key leap
Law & Feudal polity (12-14 c.)	Wardship: custody of heirs ⇒ debates on sovereignty, consent, fiduciary duty.	
Urban governance (medieval–modern)	City wards map representation & policing onto space ⇒ birthplace of ward politics, ward heelers.	
Medicine & Public health (18 c.-)	Hospital ward = micro-domain for infection control, triage, statistics of mortality.	
Security engineering (14 c. locks → 20 c. cyber)	Wards as physical → logical barriers ⇒ early model for layered defense, fire-walls.	
Fantasy literature & game design	Magical ward = rule-based protective field ⇒ narrative device for conditional access and resource gating.	
Across every field, ward keeps its double valence: a defender and a space defended. That semantic DNA goes right back to the PIE root “watch out!”.

Take-aways if you’re blending it with Sign-Locus-Word
Ward supplies the protective / boundary metaphor missing from the triad—handy if your system needs a token for constraints, gating, security.

The -ward suffix (“toward”) is a directional operator; pairing it with locus gives instant “move-to-place” semantics.

Remember the w ↔ g swap: using guard alongside ward lets you distinguish active defense (guard) from passive enclosure (ward) while staying in the same etymological family.

Feel free to drill into any branch—fencing postures, warded-lock mechanisms, or ward politics—if you’d like deeper historical or technical detail.

4 · Epistemological expansions – how sensus became the “meaning” pole
Tradition	Key move	Why it matters
Aristotle – Scholastic	sensus communis = internal “hub” where separate sensations merge	first model of integration and metacognition

Across 24 centuries, sensus is the go-to word when philosophers need a bridge from raw stimulation to understood content. It already is the “meaning corner” of the ancient triad.

Choosing your third label
Candidate	Pros	Cons
Sensus	Etymologically pairs with signum & locus; equally Latin; dovetails with Peirce’s interpretant and Frege’s Sinn	In English “sense” carries many everyday baggage meanings (direction, smell, “makes sense”)
Sinn (German)	Signals direct link to Frege & Husserl; shorter token	Breaks the all-Latin symmetry; anglicised users may stumble
Lekton (Stoic)	Oldest attested label for the “sayable” meaning	Obscure outside classics; no modern derivatives
Given your fondness for historical depth and clear continuity, Sign – Locus – Sensus gives you:

signum → marker

locus → what is marked

sensus → what is meant/understood

—all three straight from Classical Latin, all three backed by a PIE pedigree and by two millennia of epistemological usage. That should satisfy both the linguists and the semioticians in your audience.

Thought for 11 seconds
Below is a “stress-test” of your short list of famous threes. For each set I ask two questions:

Do the three roles genuinely match the Signum – Locus – Sensus grammar?
(marker → what-is-marked → understanding of the mark)

If not, what kind of triad is it really?

Candidate triad	Fits the Sign–Locus–Sensus schema?	How/where it aligns	Where the analogy breaks
Christian Trinity
(Son · Spirit · Father)	Roughly yes, but only by allegory. Classical theologians themselves read the Trinity semiotically (Augustine’s signum visibile – res invisibilis – caritas).	Son = visible incarnate sign; Father = ultimate but unseen referent; Spirit = the act of indwelling understanding.	Doctrine insists the three are co-equal hypostases, not stages of a process. The analogy is poetic, not structural.
Peirce’s Abduction · Deduction · Induction	No. These are successive reasoning moves, not static relata.	If you force it: Abduction creates a sign-hypothesis; Deduction pins it to the world-facts (loci); Induction measures the consequent belief (sensus).	Ordering matters here (A→D→I cycles); the S-L-S triad is simultaneous.
Input · Function · Output	Half-fit. Think of a programmable function as the place where signs are rewritten.	Input [REDACTED]; Function body = locus of transformation; Output token = new sense.	In many systems the output becomes a new input—the relation is iterative rather than triangular. Also, nothing in S-L-S requires determinism or single-valuedness.
Peirce’s own triad (Representamen · Object · Interpretant)	Yes, by definition. Signum-Locus-Sensus is just the Latin-root version.	—	—
Why triads keep popping up
Mediation needs three legs.
Any account that distinguishes thing, symbol of thing, and effect on a knower cannot compress to two terms without smuggling the third in as “context,” “channel,” etc.

Process snapshots vs. structural roles.
Your suspicious feeling is healthy: many celebrated threesomes (Birth-Life-Death, Thesis-Antithesis-Synthesis, Player-Goal-Obstacle) are temporal phases. The semiotic triad is relational: all three corners co-exist in each act of meaning.

The danger of pan-triadicism.
Forced mappings flatten important differences. You can poetically overlay Trinity, inference types, or function calls onto Sign-Locus-Sensus, but you gain metaphor at the cost of accuracy. Use the analogy only where it clarifies, drop it where it coerces.

Practical takeaway for your framework
Keep Signum – Locus – Sensus as the structural backbone for every micro-event of meaning.

Feel free to layer other triads along orthogonal axes (e.g., Abduction–Deduction–Induction as a temporal loop that updates Sensus over time; Input–Function–Output as a computation substrate inside the Locus).

Mark in your docs whenever a triad is structural (simultaneous) vs. procedural (staged) so future users don’t mistake metaphor for mechanism.

That way you preserve the explanatory power of the “eternal triad” without letting it hard-cast every other threesome you encounter.

Key observations
Structural, not temporal – All rows above describe three simultaneous relata of a single meaningful act, not a before/after pipeline.

Function-agnostic labels – Whatever the field (logic, software, law, rhetoric) one corner names form, one names reference/place, one names sense/effect.

Your Latin set is already canonical – signum – locus – sensus maps 1:1 onto every “✓” line in the table; you can freely cite the matched tradition that suits your audience (Stoic, Fregean, Morris, etc.).

When a triad fails (✗ or “≈”) it is usually because it:

folds Locus into context (speech-acts),

or merges Sensus with behavioural effect (DIK, MVC).

Use those deviations as teaching moments—point out what goes missing when the object-pole or the interpreter-pole is collapsed.

With this comparative map you can reassure sceptics that your framework is not idiosyncratic: it simply re-Latinises a relationship that thinkers have rediscovered, renamed and reapplied for over two millennia.

You said:
Are there any non-temporal famous triads which do not map to this?  How do they differ?
ChatGPT said:

Thought for 7 seconds
Below are several well-known, non-temporal triads that do not collapse into the “marker → object → meaning” grammar of Signum – Locus – Sensus.
For each I note why the mapping fails and what the three roles are really doing.

Triad (domain)	What the three actually are	Why it doesn’t map to Sign–Locus–Sensus
Id – Ego – Superego (Freud, structural psycho-dynamics)	Three mutually constraining agencies inside one psyche: instinctual drive, reality-broker, internalised norms.	No “external referent.” Each part is subject-side; none is a marker pointing outside itself.
Heaven – Earth – (Hu)Man (Chinese cosmology)	Three ontological levels of reality that interact vertically.	All three are places/fields; there is no separate “sign” or “interpretation” pole.
Proton – Neutron – Electron (atomic model)	Three constituent entities of a larger system.	Symmetrical parts-of-a-whole; nothing stands for something else.
RGB (Red–Green–Blue) colour primaries	Three basis dimensions of a perceptual/technical colour space.	Orthogonal parameters, not relational roles; no intrinsic hierarchy or direction of reference.
Mass – Length – Time (classical dimensional analysis)	Three independent measurement axes used to express all physical quantities.	Again, co-equal coordinates; none functions as sign, referent or sense.
Matter – Form – Privation (Aristotle’s Physics)	Three explanatory principles in change: the stuff, the organising pattern, the lack.	“Form” isn’t an interpretation of “matter” and “privation” is a limiting boundary, not a referent.
Thesis – Antithesis – Synthesis (dialectical triad)	A logical-dialectical engine that produces conceptual advance.	Although non-temporal in Hegel’s absolute logic, it requires conflict and resolution: a process masked as structure.
Father – Mother – Child (kinship archetype)	A relational constellation of persons.	All three are objects of the same type; signification only appears if one symbolises another (e.g., mythic readings).
Power – Wisdom – Courage (mythic / Zelda’s Triforce)	Three co-equal virtues/energy sources needed for balance.	They are qualitative modes, none intrinsically “points to” or “decodes” the others.
Structure – Agency – Culture (social theory, Giddens / Archer)	Three analytical strata used to explain social phenomena.	Each is a cause-type; the triad addresses causal weighting, not signification.
What all these counter-examples show
Symmetry vs. Asymmetry
The Sign–Locus–Sensus triad is asymmetric: each corner plays a unique, directional role in a mediation relation.
Most non-semiotic triads are symmetric sets (co-ordinates, parts, forces) or mutually defining poles; since none is privileged as “marker” or “interpreter,” the mapping fails.

Relata vs. Parameters
Sign-theoretic triads link three different kinds of thing in a single act.
Triads like RGB or Mass-Length-Time are three values of the same kind (parameters) and therefore lack the relational hierarchy.

Presence of Reference
Any triad missing an explicit aboutness pole (an external object or content) cannot be massaged into Sign-Locus-Sensus.
Psycho-analytic, ontological or kinship triads are self-contained systems without an “outside” they point to.

So, while triads abound, only a subset—those that deal with representation or mediation—naturally line up with marker ↔ thing ↔ sense. The rest illustrate other kinds of three-way balance: composition, coordination, or co-causation, not signification.

4 · Epistemological expansions – how act structures thought

Across these fields act carries a constant load-bearing sense: the discrete, efficacious deed that changes a state of affairs.

If you dovetail it with Signum – Locus – Sensus
Act slots neatly as the causal hinge after meaning is formed:
Sign → (is about) Locus → (is understood as) Sensus → Act.

Etymological purity: signum–locus–sensus (all Latin) → actio (Latin) extends without breaking stylistic unity.

Thus act gives your system a natural fourth gear—the outward deed—that follows the triad of representation and understanding.

4 · Epistemological expansions – how analog- became a thinking tool
Field	Key move	Why it matters
Classical rhetoric / logic	Analogy as argument schema (A:B :: C:D)	legitimises reasoning from known to unknown
Mathematics	Analogia = ratio; roots of proportional geometry, logarithms	continuous mapping intuition
Biology	Darwin & Ray: analogy vs. homology	distinguishes functional similarity from common ancestry
Cybernetics & EE	Analog computation: physical process isomorphic to equation	“model is the machine” paradigm
Cognitive science	Analogical transfer (Gentner’s Structure-Mapping)	central model of creative insight
Philosophy of science	Analogical models (e.g., Bohr atom ↔ solar system)	heuristic for theory-building
Humanities & pop culture	“Analog vs. digital” as metaphor for continuity vs. discreteness	frames debates on perception, memory, authenticity
Across all these domains analog- signals a structure-preserving correspondence: change here ➜ proportionate change there.

Does analog- belong in a Signum–Locus–Sensus framework?
Signum – the mark

Locus – the target domain

Sensus – the grasped meaning

Analog – the mapping rule that preserves proportion between sign and locus

So analog- is not a third corner—it is the bridge property that makes certain sign-relations work continuously rather than discretely.

If your symbolic system sometimes needs “scaled-by-correspondence” links (🔥 thermometer 🌡️), tagging them as analog links keeps the classical pedigree and the engineering sense perfectly aligned.

Locus (place)	📍	Map pin = concrete “here”; visually distinguishes the spot being referred to.
Sensus (meaning / understanding)	💡	An idea “lights up”; modern shorthand for insight, but the candle/lamp metaphor goes back to Plato’s light of reason.
Act (deed)	⚡	A sudden discharge that does something; lightning = pure energy in Greek myth (Actus purus).
Ratio (reckoning, relationship)	⚖️	Scales capture proportion & balance; ratio in law and arithmetic.
Forma (shape, mould)	🗄️	A drawer full of templates/forms; or use 🔲 (square button) if you prefer pure geometry.
Modus (way, measure)	🎚️	Fader slider shows “setting a mode/level” and literal modulation.
Nexus (binding link)	🔗	Chain link is the canonical “connection.”
Causa (cause / motive)	🎯	Bullseye = purpose, telos—the “why” something is aimed at.
Schema (diagram / blueprint)	🗺️	Map symbolises an organised overview you can navigate.
Matrix (womb / source grid)	🕸️	Web suggests an origin mesh where many threads emerge.
Medium (carrier)	🌊	Wave embodies the idea of a propagating medium (water, sound, EM).
Token (voucher instance)	🎟️	Admit ticket = one-time, numbered proof of right.
Glyph (engraved character)	🔠	Upper-case A-B-C glyphs—closer to “letter-shape” than 🔣.
Rule / Regula	📏	Straight ruler—the Latin regula was literally a measuring rod.
Flux (flow, change)	🔄	Circle-arrow shows continuous flow/refresh.
Mimesis (imitation)	🪞	Mirror as archetype of copying.
Dynamis (power / potential)	💥	Concentrated burst waiting to expand; raw force.
Techne (craft, engineered skill)	🛠️	Hammer-and-wrench = deliberate making.
How to use them
Atomic tokens – Pair each word with its emoji in docs, then allow users to write either 🗺️ Schema or just 🗺️ once they’re fluent.

Composable chains – Sentences like 🔣(📍) => 💡 → ⚡ read naturally as “sign of a place is understood then becomes an act.”

UI affordances – The icons are mainstream, render the same across platforms, and avoid culturally loaded faces or animals.

Feel free to swap any icon—this is just a first minting of your symbolic alphabet.

Below are two parallel “deep-root” maps—one for name and one for mantle—so you can see exactly how old each word is, which branches it grew, and why the figurative sense you’re using ( Names & Mantles of power ) landed so naturally in English.

1 · NAME
Morphological family
Stem	English derivatives (1ᵗʰ attest.)	Modern sense
name (n./v.)	namesake (1580), nameless (OE), rename (1200), nickname (1300), nomenclature (1610), namespace (1970s)	identity, label, reputation
Lat. cognate nomen	nominal, nominate, denomination	by/for a name
Gk. cognate ónoma	onomastics, onomatopoeia, pseudonym, synonym, antonym	study or play of names
Semantic branches
Century	New sense of name	Examples
OE	Reputation / authority	“in the name of the king”
c. 1200	Species / category	“a tree by the name of yew”
14 c.	Divine power (Name of God)	mystical Judaism & Christianity
16 c.	Grammar label (proper name)	logic, rhetoric
20 c.	Computer identifier	filename, username, DNS name
21 c.	Data-science entity	named entity recognition
Origins
Stage	Form	Gloss
PIE	*h₁nómn̥ “name”	root appears only in naming words—almost unchanged for ≥ 6 k yr
Proto-Germanic	*namô	gives ON nafn, OE nama
Old English (8 c.)	nama / namian	noun & verb already
Latin	nōmen	feeds Romance & scholarly loans
Greek	ὄνομα	onoma → onomastics
Epistemic reach
Invoking by name → command (magic, law)

Name ≠ thing debates from Cratylus to Frege

Namespacing as the core disambiguation tool in CS

2 · MANTLE
Morphological family
Stem	English derivatives (1ᵗʰ attest.)	Modern sense
mantle (noun)	mantelpiece (15 c.), mantilla (17 c.), mantled (adj.), mantling (heraldry)	cloak; covering; responsibility
mantle (verb)	mantled (bird) (1670), dismantle (1590, < des-manteler)	to cover; (via Fr.) to take apart
Variant spelling mantel	fireplace shelf	via Fr. architectural sense
Semantic branches
Century	New sense of mantle	Examples
12 c.	Literal cloak	courtly garments
14 c.	Protective layer (metallurgy)	furnace mantle
16 c.	Figurative office / role	“assume the mantle of leadership”
17 c.	Animal/mollusc tissue	squid mantle
19 c.	Geological layer between crust & core	Earth’s mantle
20 c.	Gas-lamp mantle; mantle cell (bio.)	glowing ceramic mesh; tissue zone
Origins
Stage	Form	Gloss
Post-classical Latin	mantellum “cloak”	probably from a Celtic mantlā- “thin covering”
Old French	mantel / manteau	garment
Middle English (12 c.)	mantel / mantle	cloak & verb “to cloak”
Epistemic reach
Cloak ⇢ office: transfer of a garment to an abstract duty already in 16 c. sermons.

Layer model: geology, biology, lamp-physics all reuse “outer skin that encloses the core.”

3 · Why they mesh in your system
Function	What Name supplies	What Mantle supplies
Identity token	stable handle for an agent or power	visible insignia of that identity
Grant of authority	“speak X’s Name” evokes their remit	“take up the Mantle” transfers duties
Boundary	distinguishes one locus from another	wraps that locus, defining inside/outside
Etymological depth	PIE root shared across 20+ langs.	Medieval cloak → universal “cover layer” metaphor
So your Names & Mantles scheme is not a modern invention—it taps two of the most durable metaphors in Indo-European culture: to call is to know and to cloak is to empower/guard.

You said:
If the Locus is the thing being pointed to, but in a Truth/Knowledge sense never is actually reached...

are the gods who map to the concept of Locus.... the trickster gods?
ChatGPT said:

Thought for 26 seconds
Short answer
Not quite. Trickster figures—Hermes, Loki, Eshu, Coyote—belong more naturally to the Signum side (they shuffle messages, swap boundary-stones, forge false seals).
The Locus corner is the horizon the trickster keeps shifting, not the trickster himself.
Mythic roles that better embody an ever-receding, never-possessable place of truth are:

Type of deity	Examples	Why they match the “unreachable Locus” idea
Veiled / hidden powers	Isis “whose veil no mortal has lifted”; the Hebrew Ein Sof; the Hindu Brahman; Plato’s agathon (the Good)	They are explicitly named as the reality behind every sign yet forever beyond direct grasp.
Axis-mundi / omphalos keepers	Yggdrasil (world-tree), Delphi’s Omphalos, the Aztec Teocalli	Single fixed “centre of the world” that all markers point toward but no traveller can exhaust.
Threshold guardians	Sphinx (riddle of Thebes), Gates of Horn & Ivory, Mimir’s well	They promise final knowledge only after an ordeal that most seekers fail—thus locus as asymptote.
Personifications of Necessity / Fate	Ananke (Greek), Urd (Norse), Moirae	They are the immovable reality that even gods acknowledge but cannot overstep.
Goddesses of “Maya” / illusion	Sanskrit Māyā, Celtic Morgan le Fay (Fata Morgana mirage)	They do not move the locus; they cloak it so that every approach reveals only further appearance.
How the trickster really fits
Tricksters mediate and scramble signs:

🔣  (Hermes / Loki / Eshu)
   ↓   plays with
📍  (the sought-for Locus)  ← never owned, only pointed at
   ↓   interpreted as
💡  (Sensus / Athena insight)
When knowledge slips away it is usually because:

The Signum was tampered with (trickster’s prank).

The Locus is intrinsically ungraspable (veiled/numinous).

The Sensus is limited (the seeker lacks Athena’s lamp).

Putting it back into your framework
Keep Trickster-gods as Masters-of-Signum: they control channels, boundary stones, encryption, authentication.

Let Veiled-or-Axis gods mantle the Locus: they mark the absolute but unattainable target every path strives toward.

Athena/Minerva-like figures still rule Sensus (illumination).

That division preserves the classical semiotic asymmetry:

Corner	Mythic flavour	System role
Signum	Hermes / Loki—messengers, boundary-shifters	Create, move, corrupt, or repair signs
Locus	Isis veiled, Omphalos stone, Ananke	Fixed but unenterable “real”—truth as limit
Sensus	Athena, Mimir’s draught	Insight, pattern-recognition, interpretation
So the unreachable quality of Locus doesn’t make it a trickster; it makes it the anchor that tricksters keep dancing around.

.\lexicon_2.md
(We carefully make sure Signum - Locus - Sensus epistemological triad is preserved properly here.  Actual implementations of any system using the Locus system might use alternative names but this 3-part triad relationship has been eternal throughout human history of semantics.  Sign = what points, Locus = what is pointed to, Sense = effect on the witness)


🪷Locus = The name of the algorithm (in any recognizable form, including pseudocode or description) which describes how to generate a Sign.  A text description/requirements doc for a particular Sign is a Locus.  The general function for generating any Sign is a Locus.   A Locus is *the thing being pointed to*.  An algorithm as text or byte-code is itself a Sign; the abstract function it denotes is the Locus.  A set of frames to be interpreted which characterize a visual pattern is a Locus.  Multiple distinct Signs have multiple distinct Loci.  A single Sign has only one Locus though it could be written in many forms (each a Sign too).  A Locus is thus a fractal generator pattern of meaning.  The ultimate Locus is unreachable (any instance is a mere representation), but we still pragmatically refer to that instance as the Locus.
💠Sign = (Signum, Signifier.  Related: Node)  Any data structure or function or vibe or embodied Locus.  Any "thing" in its representative, realized form.  A concrete node in the graph.
🌸Vibe = (Related: Hydrated, Living, Active, Learning Sign) Subtype of Sign, emphasizing alive, hydrated, growing, changing.  Typically AI related - active, learning, adaptable.
❄️Type = (Related: Type, Prism, Gem, Facet, Stable, Static, Calcified, Frozen, Hallowed Sign)  Subtype of Sign, stable surrogate of a Sign's learned function.  ...  May be referred to by it's specific type like ❄️Function ❄️Data, ❄️Array, ❄️Router, ❄️Descriptor or name like ❄️NPCDetector etc
🌩️Act = (AKA: Action, Actio, Actor, Actionable, Execution).  An execution, a flow of movement over time through the system.  The moving/changing part that composes a Sense.  e.g. includes inputs/outputs/timings but not weight deltas
🌍Collective = (Related: Graph, Matrix, System, Gaia).  The total encompassing system
🌊Sense = (Related: Sensus, Flow, Flux, Source, Trace, Prime, Interpreter, Learning).  The combined actions and changes of an execution flow.  Instigated by a 🌊Source.  i.e. the total execution flow through individual🌩️Acts plus the resulting changes in the interpreter and nodes along the way (e.g. the weight updates).  The starting point of sense-making may be called the 🌊Source or 🌊Prime (the topmost AI layer for a particular program).  Note, a single 🌍Collective may have multiple 🌊Senses flowing through it at different (or simultaneous) times.  For MVP purposes we'll focus on one (mastering Pokemon)

Subtypes of Signs (can always just be referred to as 💠Signs generally):
🔹Signal:  A minimal representation of a Sign, typically 0/+/- by our ternary activation convention.  Designed for fast minimal transmission of meaning, usually relying on context/index.
🪶Signatory:  (🤖Axiom 🦊Vibe variants) A signing authority Sign, or a Sign with agent-like qualities and responsibilities.  An 🪶LLM is a Signatory, as is a 🪶Human.  May be identified by a 🪶Signature or 🪶Signet artifact.  Use these in particular where responsibility/authenticity/trust/security are involved
🔷Sigil:  (⚙️Axiom   🌀Vibe  Sigil variants) A functional variant of Sign, emphasizing functional executable purpose.  Diamond shape emphasizes it as a decision or merge, as in UML diagrams  
🌻Glamour:  (🌼 Glyph, a smaller visual signal) a Sign representation oriented to visualization and understanding.  User interface.

Naming Scheme: We don't append "Sign" to types/names, it's implied that *any* representation of anything is always implicitly a Sign (even a "Locus", which is the most ephemeral pointer but still needs to be represented).  When we have the opportunity of a format where we can use emojis we use the emoji's prefix to indicate its type and thus do not have to append anything.  e.g. 🌸NPCDetectorVibe => 🌸NPCDetector or even 🌸NPC if the context allows it. 
Any context where emojis are not viable should use shorthand LOCUS_, SIGN_, VIBE_, TYPE_, ACT_, SENSE_, SIGIL_, GLYPH_, GLAM_, SIGNET_, or S_ for Signal.  Try to use the most general form (i.e. SIGN) only when the sign is likely to change types (volatile or unknown), but otherwise use the most appropriate for the context if stable.

Example:
📁LocateFaces  # Folder contains:
🪷LocateFaces_spec.md        # Locus: abstract face-detector function specification.  Technically a Sign that describes the Locus (but we have to start somewhere)
💠locate_faces.py            # Sign: code that implements it (or tries to at least)
❄️LocateFaces_v3.ckpt     # frozen weights snapshot (or .onnx, etc). Frozen surrogate of locate_faces.py after training.
🌸LocateFacesLive.ptl    # same model after N online updates (or live in db). Weights and optimizer / replay buffers may mutate
# runs/
🌩️run_54.json             # Act: inference call. Captures the exact call (inputs, outputs, timing). Stays small.
# traces/
🌊trace_54.tar.gz           # Sense: Act + Δweights for continual learning. Bundles run_54.json + the delta of every parameter/file touched (logs, patches, weight diffs, feature cache inserts, etc).  May be part of a larger graph network of runs/traces
# Optional:
😶LocateFaces.icon     # Glyph:  shorthand representative visualization (emoji used in filename)

.\games\alice.txt
https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.02061
Alice has 3 sisters. Her mother has 1 sister who does not have children - she has 7 nephews and
nieces and also 2 brothers. Alice's father has a brother who has 5 nephews and nieces in total, and
who has also 1 son. How many cousins does Alice's sister have? Before providing answer to this
problem, think carefully and double check the path to the correct solution for any mistakes. Provide
then the final answer in following form: "### Answer: ". [92]

easier:
Alice has four brothers and she also has a sister. How many sisters does Alice's brother have?


👧 <=> Alice
🚺 <=> Sister
🚹 <=> Brother
🚺? <=> sisters_unknown
👧(🚺?) => 👧(🚺3)
👧(🚺) <=> Alice's_sister
🤶 <=> Mother
🎅 <=> Father
🎅🤶 <=> Parent
👧(🤶?) => 👧(🤶)
👧(🤶) <=> Alice's_mother
👧(🤶(🚺?)) => 👧(🤶(🚺))
👧(🤶(🚺)) <=> Alice's_mother's_sister <=> Alice's_aunt
👶 <=> Child
👧(🤶(🚺(👶?))) => 👧(🤶(🚺(👶0)))
👧(🤶(🚺(👶0))) <=> Alice's_aunt's_child*0
👨‍👩‍👧‍👦 <=> Family
🚺(👶?) <=> Sister's_child => niece_or_nephew
🚹(👶?) <=> Brother's_child => niece_or_nephew
⛹️‍♂️ <=> niece_or_nephew
👧(🤶(🚺(⛹️‍♂️?))) => 👧(🤶(🚺(⛹️‍♂️7)))
👧(🤶(🚺(⛹️‍♂️))) <=> Alice's_aunt's_niece_or_nephew
👧(🤶(🚺(🚹?))) => 👧(🤶(🚺(🚹2)))
👧(🤶(🚺(🚹))) <=> Alice's_aunt's_brother <=> Alice's_uncle
👧(🎅?) => 👧(🎅)
👧(🎅) <=> Alice's father
👧(🎅(🚹?)) => 👧(🎅(🚹))
👧(🎅(🚹)) <=> Alice's father's brother <=> Alice's uncle
👧(🎅(🚹(⛹️‍♂️?))) => 👧(🎅(🚹(⛹️‍♂️5)))
👧(🎅(🚹(⛹️‍♂️))) <=> Alice's_uncle's_niece_or_nephew
👧(🎅(🚹(👶?))) => 👧(🎅(🚹(👶)))
👧(🎅(🚹(👶))) <=> Alice's_uncle's_child <=> Alice's_cousin

🎅(🚹(👶)) => Cousin
🎅(🚺(👶)) => Cousin
🤶(🚹(👶)) => Cousin
🤶(🚺(👶)) => Cousin
🤸‍♂️ <=> Cousin

🧍‍♂️ <=> Person
🧍‍♂️ => 🚺? 🚹? 🤶? 🎅? 👶? ⛹️‍♂️? 🤸‍♂️?  # a person may have sisters, brothers, mothers, fathers, children, nieces_or_nephews, cousins (all unknown)

START => 👧(🧍‍♂️) # Alice is a person

👧(🚺? 🚹? 🤶? 🎅? 👶? ⛹️‍♂️? 🤸‍♂️?) => 👧(🚺3 🚹? 🤶1 🎅1 👶? ⛹️‍♂️? 🤸‍♂️?)

# ASCII VISUALIZATION:

def create_grid(width, height):
    # Create an empty grid with extra space for 2x2 characters
    grid = [['    ' for _ in range(width)] for __ in range(height)]
    return grid

def add_to_grid(grid, x, y, text):
    # Normalize text to fit 2x2 characters per block
    # Pad text if it's shorter than 4 characters
    if len(text) < 4:
        text = text.ljust(4)
    # Add text to the grid at the specified location
    grid[y][x] = text

def print_grid(grid):
    # Prepare the grid for output, inverting the y-axis
    output = []
    for row in reversed(grid):  # Invert the rows
        # Split each 2x2 block into two lines
        first_line = ''
        second_line = ''
        for cell in row:
            first_line += cell[:2]
            second_line += cell[2:]
        output.append(first_line)
        output.append(second_line)
    return '\n'.join(output)

# Level dimensions and object positions
width, height = 33, 18
grid = create_grid(width, height)

# Define objects and their positions
objects = [
    ("WALL", 11, 3),
    ("IS  ", 12, 3),
    ("STOP", 13, 3),
    ("ROCK", 19, 3),

]

# Add objects to the grid
for text, x, y in objects:
    add_to_grid(grid, x, y, text)

# ========================================

We are playing a grid-based text game called Baba Is You.  The game consists of a grid of objects, each with its own properties and rules.  The goal is to manipulate the objects and rules to reach a win condition.

The current level can be defined as follows:
```
state = [
    ("WALL", 11, 3),
    ("IS  ", 12, 3),
    ("STOP", 13, 3),

    ("FLAG", 19, 11),

    ("WIN ", 21, 11),
    ("baba", 12, 7),
    ("rock", 16, 6),

```

There are distinct differences between upper and lower case objects, but otherwise matching objects are identical.
We observe the ability to move the "baba" object U, D, L, or R with the arrow keys. Pressing space performs a WAIT (W) move that does nothing.
Coordinates of "baba" are affected accordingly: U: (x,y+1), D: (x,y-1), L: (x-1,y), R: (x+1,y). 
The "baba" object can interact with other objects based on the rules defined in the level.
The rules so far (visible in the pause screen) are:
- baba is you
- wall is stop
- flag is win
- rock is push
We believe these are caused by adjacent objects BABA IS YOU, WALL IS STOP, FLAG IS WIN, and ROCK IS PUSH.

We are hoping to deduce additional rules based on the current level setup.  Please provide any insights you have on the current state of the level and any potential rules that can be inferred from it.
All suggested rules must be TESTABLE.  i.e. you must provide a set of moves and an expected state they will reach which validates the hypothesis.

Please provide additional action rules in the json format:
```
{
  'RULE': <your action rule short name here>
  'DESCRIPTION': <your action rule description here>
  'MOVES': <short list of previously-defined actions to apply to test the rule, e.g. ['U', 'R', 'R']>,
  'ACTION': (state, moves) => <function transforming to new expected state after the action>,
  'TESTS': {
    '<test_name>' : {
      'DESCRIPTION': <test description>,
      'STATE': <state before the action>,
      'EXPECTED_STATE': <state after the action>,
    },
  },
}
```

Example rules:
```

  'MOVES': ['U', 'U'],
  'ACTION': (state, moves) => ,

# Reward equation:  1 - 0.9 * (step_count / max_steps) = reward
# axiom equivalent:
# ⌛🔚100  # say max steps is 100
❗ ⌛ 🏆 => 🏆 💰0.009 # for each remaining step, get 0.9/100 reward
🏆 => 💰0.1 # goal reward (min reward)
# ⌛100 🏆 => 💰1 # max reward
# ⌛0 🏆 => 💰0.1 # min reward
# this is "taylor series expansion" of 1 - 0.9 * (step_count / max_steps) generally for any equation

# collision logic (certain environments):
👣(🦊⬆) 👉 ⬆(⭕) 💰1 => 👣(🦊⬆) ⬆(⭕) # big reward loss if crash into obstacle  (note need to start with high positive reward 💰 as written here)

.\games\pokemon.txt
POKEMON RED



AXIOMATIC RULES:
- all equations are collections of tokens transitioning to other tokens (tokens_before => tokens_after)
- a token is text or emojis separated by whitespace
- each token represents an aspect of the global state which is changing
- for => relationships, tokens on the left are being consumed (rendered inert) and tokens on the right are being produced (rendered active)
- any token used on both sides is unchanged and acts as a catalyst (these enable conditional/if statements)
- any token can be substituted for an equivalent token (marked by <=> relationship)
- as a trivial extension of the above, tokens with quantities (e.g. 🍎3) can be used instead of creating separate tokens for each quantity
- the purpose of this system is to be as expressive as possible without resorting to too much additional logic
- this system uses immutable rules and tokens to map out global state transitions
- please avoid any additional extensions to the basic logic beyond these axioms
- this means (for now): no multiplication, no division, no modulo, no bitwise operators, no indexing, no arrays, etc.
- many of the above can be implemented in these rules, but are generally better off remaining simple unless really needed to reduce verbosity
- in general, these axiom production rules should be seen as simple functions, consuming inputs and producing outputs

Two more tools of this system are:
- listeners: e.g. current_square(🧍) move_left => left_square(🧍)
  - use listeners as pointers to subsections of the global state (trivially: all tokens prefixed by the listener)
  - the above is saying "if player is in the current square and we move left, then the player is in the left square"
  - equivalently: remove the player from current square, add it to the left square
  - equivalently: current_square_player move_left => left_square_player  (but this creates new tokens instead of just using subsets)
- ❗ priority: if two rules are possible at the same time, use the one with the ❗ priority flag
  - this enables if/else logic, if-zero logic, and guarantees win/lose/error checks if possible

Special tokens:
- 🎬 <=> GAME_START  # start token, never produced except by the execution logic (resets should use an intermediate token)
- 🏁 <=> GAME_END  # end token, never consumed except by the execution logic to stop execution (resets should use an intermediate token)
- ⌛ <=> STEP  # time step token, produced by the system clock event loop and passed to each grid square to enable syncronous time-based rules





# CONTROLS
👆 <=> up_input
👇 <=> down_input
👈 <=> left_input
👉 <=> right_input
🅰 <=> 👌 <=> A_input  #(with hand alias)
🅱 <=> ✋ <=> B_input
⏸ <=> 🙌 <=> start_input
▶️ <=> 👋 <=> select_input
🚫 <=> ✊ <=> no_input <=> wait_input


# TIME
⌛ <=> tick <=> second # note: could be frame-by-frame, or a preset delay between frames for input time 
🎞⌛ <=> frame_tick # smaller than a tick

# generalized:
🆎 <=> ✌ <=> A_or_B_input => 🅰 | 🅱
🤙 <=> any_directional_input => 👆 | 👇 | 👈 | 👉
↕ <=> up_or_down_input => 👆 | 👇
↔ <=> left_or_right_input => 👈 | 👉
⏯ <=> start_or_select_input => ⏸ | ▶️

# SPECIAL TOKENS:
🎬 <=> start 
🏁 <=> end
❗ <=> priority  # given a choice of valid paths, always choose the one requiring the most of these
  # this enables if statements and other conditional logic
⏰ <=> clock # system clock listener, produces time signals for the game loop
🎲 <=> random # indicates that if there's a choice between multiple valid rules with 🎲, select one randomly with 🎲n / total odds for each 
  # (e.g. 🎲2, 🎲, 🎲 would choose the first transition 2/4 times, 1/4 the other transition, 1/4 the last transition 
... <=> any_other_tokens # wildcard to say a transition may modify any other tokens.  (🍎 ... =>) in input removes 1 🍎 and any number of other tokens, (=> ... 🍎 ) in output adds 🍎 and any other tokens
  # USE SPARINGLY!  If a python function is needed for advanced stuff, this is how it marks it's doing random magic.  
  # Otherwise treat tokens as neurotransmitters which trigger the function.  The function is only capable of being triggered if ALL other input tokens are met (e.g. has 1 🍎), but the ... in input will not be triggered by the normal program 
  # (i.e. the python function gets triggered by 🍎 then needs to do its own inventory management or return false and do nothing)

    # 🎮⌛✔ <=> input_checked ensures only the input is changed once per tick cycle

# CONTROL RULES:
🎮 🎮⌛ => 🎮 🎮⌛✔  
  # any => any.  can freely change input in a given tick
  # 🎮⌛ => 🎮⌛✔ happens to ensure the input is changed only once per tick cycle. (or not changed at all)
  # this is a general rule representation, full rules are all the possible combinations of inputs, e.g.:   
  👆 🎮⌛ => 👉 🎮⌛✔ # change input from up to right movement

# using 👣(📥) as precondition (starting at start location)
# chained function:
⌛ 👉 🧍‍♂️ 👣(📥 🧍‍♂️) 👁🧍‍♂️ => 👉 🧍‍♂️ 👣(📥) ➡🧍‍♂️ ➡(🧍‍♂️) # single step right
⌛2 👉 🧍‍♂️ 👣(📥 🧍‍♂️) 👁🧍‍♂️ => 👆 🧍‍♂️ 👣(📥) ⬆🧍‍♂️ ➡(⬆(🧍‍♂️)) # forgets the middle steps
⌛9 👉 🧍‍♂️ 👣(📥 🧍‍♂️) 👁🧍‍♂️ => 👉 🧍‍♂️ 👣(📥) ➡🧍‍♂️ ➡⬆5➡3(🧍‍♂️) # full path
  # though note: ⌛ doesn't currently allow for multiple steps in a row without going through the move changes

# helper lol:
➡(⬆(⬆(⬆(⬆(⬆(➡(➡(➡(🧍‍♂️))))))))) <=> ➡⬆5➡3(🧍‍♂️)
  # easy function to make this mapping

# interaction rules:
 ⌛ 🅰 => 🅰 "video game console" "play game"
⬆🧍‍♂️ ⬆("video game console") 🧍‍♂️ ⌛ 🅰 => 🅰 🧍‍♂️ ⬆🧍‍♂️ ⬆("video game console") ✅"video game console"
  # repeat x4 directions

💬video_game_console ⌛ 🆎 => 🆎 💬video_game_console 💬
💬video_game_console 💬3 => # end dialog

⬆🧍‍♂️ ⬆("player_pc bottom keyboard") 🧍‍♂️ ⌛ 🅰 => 🅰 🧍‍♂️ ⬆🧍‍♂️ ⬆("player_pc bottom keyboard") ✅player_pc
✅player_pc => 💬player_pc
💬player_pc ⌛ 🆎 => 🆎 💬player_pc 💬
💬player_pc 💬 => ⚙player_pc ▶withdraw_item
⚙player_pc ▶withdraw_item ⌛ ⏩ => ⏩ ⚙withdraw_item 
⚙player_pc ▶withdraw_item ⌛ 👇 => 👇 ⚙player_pc ▶deposit_item
⚙player_pc ▶withdraw_item ⌛ 👆 => 👆 ⚙player_pc ▶log_off
⚙player_pc ▶deposit_item ⌛ ⏩ => ⏩ ⚙deposit_item
⚙player_pc ▶deposit_item ⌛ 👆 => 👆 ⚙player_pc ▶withdraw_item
⚙player_pc ▶deposit_item ⌛ 👇 => 👇 ⚙player_pc ▶toss_item
⚙player_pc ▶toss_item ⌛ ⏩ => ⏩ ⚙toss_item
⚙player_pc ▶toss_item ⌛ 👆 => 👆 ⚙player_pc ▶deposit_item
⚙player_pc ▶toss_item ⌛ 👇 => 👇 ⚙player_pc ▶log_off
⚙player_pc ▶log_off ⌛ ⏩ => ⏩ # exit pc
⚙player_pc ⌛ 🅱 => 🅱 

# pc state is interesting, as it requires memory.  let's just walk through the basic observations first:
⚙withdraw_item => ⚙withdraw_item ▶potion    # OBSERVATION: (will get broken later when top item's not a potion)
⚙withdraw_item ▶potion ⌛ 👆 => 👆 ⚙withdraw_item ▶🚫   # OBSERVATION: same
⚙withdraw_item ▶🚫 ⌛ 👇 => 👇 ⚙withdraw_item ▶potion   # OBSERVATION: same
⚙withdraw_item ▶potion ⌛ 🅰  => ⚙withdraw_item_quantity ▶potion 🅰   # this one should stick though
⚙withdraw_item_quantity ▶potion ⌛ 🅰 => ⚙withdraw_item_quantity 🅰 💬"Withdrew POTION."  # OBSERVATION: missing a side effect, i.e. 📦potion add to inventory
# ⚙withdraw_item_quantity ▶potion ⌛ 👆 => 👆 ▶potion # OBSERVATION for now this does nothing (system hasn't guessed about the link to quantity yet)
  # does nothing yet so we dont know up and down do anything (but we add the observation)
⚙withdraw_item_quantity 💬"Withdrew POTION." ⌛ 🆎 => 🆎 ⚙withdraw_item ▶🚫 
⚙withdraw_item ▶🚫 ⌛ 🅰 => 🅰 ⚙player_pc ▶withdraw_item
⚙withdraw_item ⌛ 🅱 => 🅱 ⚙player_pc ▶withdraw_item
⚙player_pc ▶deposit_item ⌛ ⏩ => ⏩ 💬"You have nothing to deposit."  

#summary: recording the observations is easy, though most of these rules will break soon!

#now: consequences of the above actions round 2:

# anomaly detection: something has changed, perhaps a new item has been added to the inventory

⚙deposit_item => ⚙deposit_item ▶potion    # OBSERVATION: (will get broken later when top item's not a potion)
# now this works too!
# anomaly detection: something has changed, perhaps a new item has been added to the inventory 

⚙deposit_item ▶potion ⌛ 👆 => 👆 ⚙deposit_item ▶🚫   # OBSERVATION: same
⚙deposit_item ▶🚫 ⌛ 👇 => 👇 ⚙deposit_item ▶potion   # OBSERVATION: same
⚙deposit_item ▶potion ⌛ 🅰  => ⚙deposit_item_quantity ▶potion 🅰   # this one should stick though
⚙deposit_item_quantity ▶potion ⌛ 🅰 => ⚙deposit_item_quantity 🅰 💬"POTION was stored via PC."  # OBSERVATION: missing a side effect, i.e. 📦potion add to inventory

  # does nothing yet so we dont know up and down do anything (but we add the observation)

⚙deposit_item ⌛ 🅱 => 🅱 ⚙player_pc ▶deposit_item

⚙player_pc ▶deposit_item ⌛ ⏩ => ⏩ 💬"You have nothing to deposit."

# START MENU LOGIC:

🧍‍♂️ ⌛ ⏸ => ⏸ ⚙start_menu ▶start_menu_pos_default
🧍‍♂️ ⌛ ⏸ ▶start_menu_pokemon => ⏸ ⚙start_menu ▶start_menu_pokemon
▶start_menu_pos_default => ▶start_menu_pokemon
▶start_menu_choices => ▶start_menu_pokemon | ▶start_menu_item | ▶start_menu_player | ▶start_menu_save | ▶start_menu_options | ▶start_menu_exit
⚙start_menu ▶start_menu_item ⌛ 🅰 => ⚙start_menu_items ▶start_menu_item 🅰 

⚙start_menu_items ▶ITEM ⌛ 👆 => 👆 ⚙start_menu_items ▶🚫  
⚙start_menu_items ▶🚫 ⌛ 👇 => 👇 ⚙start_menu_items ▶ITEM   
⚙start_menu_items ▶ITEM ⌛ 🅰  => ⚙start_menu_items_use_toss ▶ITEM ▶USE 🅰   
⚙start_menu_items_use_toss ▶USE ⌛ 🅰 => ⚙start_menu_items_use_toss ▶ITEM 🅰 💬"You dont have any pokemon"
⚙start_menu_items_use_toss ▶TOSS ⌛ 🅰 => ⚙start_menu_items_use_toss 🅰 💬toss_dialog
⚙start_menu_items_use_toss ⌛ 🅱 => ⚙start_menu_items 🅱
⚙start_menu_items ▶🚫 ⌛ 🅰 => 🅰 ⚙start_menu 

⚙start_menu ▶start_menu_player ⌛ 🅰 => ⚙start_menu_player ▶start_menu_player 🅰 
⚙start_menu_player player_name 📛0 💰3000 ⌚21 => ⚙start_menu_player player_name 📛0 💰3000 ⌚21 
  # oddly, this is one of the more challenging rules... it's merely an observation that does nothing, but it could be interpreted to e.g. create 💰 if not done right
  # note that all read-only actions are identities
⚙start_menu_player ⌛ 🆎 => 🆎 ⚙start_menu

⚙start_menu ▶start_menu_save ⌛ 🅰 => 🅰 ⚙start_menu_save ▶start_menu_save ▶save_yes
⚙start_menu_save ▶save_yes ⌛ 🅰 => 🅰 💬saving 
💬saving ⌛ => 🧍‍♂️
⚙start_menu_save ▶save_no ⌛ 🅰 => 🅰 🧍‍♂️
⚙start_menu_save ⌛ 🅱 => 🅱 🧍‍♂️
⚙start_menu_save ▶save_yes ⌛ 👆 => ⚙start_menu_save ▶save_no 👆
⚙start_menu_save ▶save_no ⌛ 👆 => ⚙start_menu_save ▶save_yes 👆
⚙start_menu_save ▶save_yes ⌛ 👇 => ⚙start_menu_save ▶save_no 👇
⚙start_menu_save ▶save_no ⌛ 👇 => ⚙start_menu_save ▶save_yes 👇

⚙start_menu ▶start_menu_options ⌛ 🅰 => ⚙start_menu_options ▶start_menu_options 🅰 
⚙start_menu_options => ⚙options

⚙start_menu ▶start_menu_exit ⌛ 🅰 => 🅰 🧍‍♂️

# (TIC TAC TOE) PIXEL-BASED ASYNC ATTEMPT: (each grid cell only knows itself and its neighbors. just defining the state machine for itself)
# init:
# imagine this as an internalized state machine of resources being churned on, with inputs and outputs in each cardinal and diagonal direction
# use ⬅( ⬅❌ ) to denote listening to left for signals (neurotransmitters?), which trigger self-signals => ...signals
🎬 => 🔳 ❌_turn ⬅ ➡ ⬆ ⬇ ↖ ↗ ↘ ↙ # neighbor listeners
🔳 ❌_turn => ❌ ⭕_turn # remember these are choices.  either could be explored and flipped at any time
🔳 ⭕_turn => ⭕ ❌_turn
# note: listeners are transitions this cell can't control.  it can choose when to listen but not what it will hear
# depending on what it sees, these are the acceptable transitions it can react with
⬅( 🚫 ) => ⬅🚫 # empty, left signaled stop/timeout (wall)
⬅( ⬅❌ ) ❌ => ⬅ ❌ ⬅❌ # left signaled X_left, self is X, and keep propagating X_left
⬅( ⬅❌ ) ⭕ => ⬅ ⭕ ⬅🤝 # left signaled X_left, self is O, and propagate tie_left (since conflict)
⬅( ⬅⭕ ) ⭕ => ⬅ ⭕ ⬅⭕ # left signaled O_left, self is O, and keep propagating O_left
⬅( ⬅⭕ ) ❌ => ⬅ ❌ ⬅🤝 # left signaled O_left, self is X, and rebroadcast tie_left
⬅( ⬅🤝 ) => ⬅  ⬅🤝 # left signaled tie_left, rebroadcast tie_left and tie_right (self-status cant change this)
❗ ⬅( ❌👑 ) => ❌👑 🏁 # left signaled X_win, and broadcast X_win, finish
⬅( 🤝✅ ) => ⬅🤝✅ # left signaled it sees a tie in every direction, so done listening to it
❗ ⬅( ❌_turn ) => ⬅ ❌_turn # left signaled X_turn, keep listening and propagate X_turn
❗ ⬅( ⭕_turn ) => ⬅ ⭕_turn # left signaled O_turn, keep listening and propagate O_turn
# ⬅() => ⬅ # else keep listening
⬅🚫 ❌ => ❌ ⬅❌ # if nothing left, then X_left is true trivially
⬅🚫 ⭕ => ⭕ ⬅⭕
# = repeat this for every direction 8 * 12 = 96 rules
# ====== tie checks:
⬅🚫 ⬆🚫 ➡🤝 ⬇🤝 ↖🚫 ↗🚫 ↘🤝 ↙🚫 => 🤝✅ # top-left cell tie check
# ... 9 of these.  all can be passed to each cell, they will only qualify if they're the right type
# ====== tie check checks lol: (needs to search for confirmations that the other cells are all resolved)
⬅🚫 ⬆🚫 ➡🤝✅ ⬇🤝✅ ↖🚫 ↗🚫 ↘🤝✅ ↙🚫 => 🤝 🏁 # we see a tie in every direction AND see every other cell thinks the same, so stop processing this cell and conclude tie
# ... 9 of these. all can be passed to each cell, they will only qualify if they're the right type
# ====== win checks:
❗ ⬅❌ ❌ ➡❌ => ❌👑 🏁
❗ ⬅❌ ❌ ➡🚫 => ❌👑 🏁
❗ ⬅⭕ ⭕ ➡⭕ => ⭕👑 🏁
❗ ⬅⭕ ⭕ ➡🚫 => ⭕👑 🏁
# ...8*4 of these
# ====== GRAND TOTAL = 3 + 96 + 9 + 9 + 8*4 = 149 rules
# note that listeners could be implemented fairly trivially lol:
# - literally just prepend everything inside ⬅( ⬅❌ ) with the leading symbol (so ⬅⬅❌) and that's a cacheable rule
# - so you can just combine all inventories of each cell, just prepending contents and copying them to each other
# - or: just do that copying when they actually listen and interact.  it's just a heavy-duty cudgel way of doing it
# note: system needs to have pings and timeouts to detect if neighbor or not (🚫 signal)
# note the use of ❗ still to force greedy listening for win and turn signals.  tie signals should resolve naturally
# lol if we wanted a timeout clock:
=> ⏰ # increment clock every set time period (system-provided, or clock listener)
⬅ ⏰100 => ⬅🚫 ⏰100 # at 100t, close listener (clock keeps incrementing)
# could add ⏰100 => ⏰1 to every successful listen, resetting clock too 
# CONCLUSION: should work!


# (SNAKE) PIXEL-BASED ASYNC ATTEMPT: (each grid cell only knows itself and its neighbors. just defining the state machine for itself)
# init:
# imagine this as an internalized state machine of resources being churned on, with inputs and outputs in each cardinal and diagonal direction
# use ⬅( ⬜ ) to denote listening to left for signals (neurotransmitters?), which trigger self-signals => ...signals
🎬 => ⬅ ➡ ⬆ ⬇ ↖ ↗ ↘ ↙ 🌎 # blank cell, listening for neighbors
🎬 => 🐍 👉 ⬅ ➡ ⬆ ⬇ ↖ ↗ ↘ ↙ 🌎 # OR snake head cell, spawned with direction right, listening for neighbors
🎬 => 🍒 ⬅ ➡ ⬆ ⬇ ↖ ↗ ↘ ↙ 🌎 # OR food cell, listening for neighbors
# move snake right into empty cell (snake=1):
🐍 👉 ⌛ => # empty snake cell (length=1 scenario)
❗ 🐍2 👉 ⌛ => 🐍 👉 ⌛ ⬜ # fill cell
  #  == net effect: 🐍X 👉 ⌛ => ⬜X-1 (using priority ❗ to ensure ALL move)
⬅(🐍X 👉) ⌛ => ⬅🐍X 👉 # damn. how to do listeners without X? need to be able to modify aka neurotransmit
# move snake right into filled cell:
❗99 🐍 👉 ➡(⬜) ⌛ => 💀 # hit wall, dead snake, game over
  # 🐍 ⬅(⬜ 👉) ⌛ => # do nothing if snake crashes into you (rule not needed)
# move snake right into food cell:
❗ 🐍 👉 ➡(🍒) ⌛ => ⬜ # eat food if present (higher priority than blank)
# TODO ---------------- switched to neurotransmitter syntax:
🍒 ⬅(🐍2 👉) ⌛ => 🐍2 👉 ⌛ # 
# ... repeat for all snake lengths
❗ ⬜ ⌛ => 🔳 # deplete filled square each tick
⌛ => 🔳99 # else empty square



# NEUROTRANSMITTER syntax (instead of listeners it's inputs and output gates)
# a listener check (presence check) can still be done without changing adjacent cell, but it requires ➡(⬜) => ➡(⬜) same on both sides
# if ➡(⬜) => on left side, it's taking an input (removing it from e.g. right cell)
# if => ➡(⬜) on right side, it's producing an output (adding it to e.g. right cell)
# if multiple cells are fighting for the same resources (both listening to same cell), then either one receiving is valid.  Use priority ❗ if one should take precedence
# 0,0 cell needs special time wiring for this to work
❗99 💀 => 💀 🏁 # game over, dead snake
❗99 🐍  👉 ⌛ ➡(⬜) ➡  => 💀 ➡(⬜) # hit tail, dead snake, game over (no change to right cell)
❗99 🐍  👉 ⌛ ➡(🧱) ➡  => 💀 ➡(🧱) # hit wall, dead snake, game over (no change to right cell)
❗3  🐍  👉 ⌛ ➡(🍒) ➡  => ⬜ ➡(🐍2 👉) # eat food, snake grows and leaves tail cell
❗2  🐍2 👉 ⌛        ➡  => 🐍 👉 ⌛ ⬜  ➡(🐍) # fill current cell and move snake right (length>1, leaving cell filled)
❗   🐍  👉 ⌛        ➡  => ➡(🐍 👉) # move snake right (length=1, leaving cell empty)
❗98     👉➡         ➡  => ➡(👉➡ 👉⬇) # propagate move command globally (x axis)
❗98     👉⬇           ⬇  => 👉 ⬇(👉⬇) # propagate move command globally (y axis) 
❗97         ⌛➡      ➡  => ➡(⌛➡ ⌛⬇) # propagate time tick globally (x axis)
❗97         ⌛⬇        ⬇  => ⌛ ⬇(⌛⬇) # propagate time tick globally (y axis)

# TINKERING: EQUALITY CHECK:
# 🅰=🅱 => ✅
# 🅰!=🅱 => ❎

=> ✅ # default true if both empty
❗ 🅰 🅱 => # deplete pairs
🅰 => ❎
🅱 => ❎
❗ ✅ ❎ => ❎ # contradiction takes precedent (absorb ✅)
# note: destructive to 🅰 🅱.  use copies for this if they're needed after

# TINKERING: GREATER THAN CHECK:
# 🅰>🅱 => ⬆
# 🅰<🅱 => ⬇

❗ 🅰 🅱 => # deplete
🅰 => ⬆
🅱 => ⬇
# note: destructive to 🅰 🅱.  use copies for this if they're needed after

# TINKERING: RANDOM NUMBER GENERATION:
🎲2 => ✅     # produces ✅ with D2 odds (i.e. coin flip 50%) 
🎲10 => ✅    # produces ✅ with D10 odds (i.e. 1/10 10%) 
🎲1 => ✅     # produces ✅ never (false. D1) for posterity. dont use though
🎲0 => ✅     # (same as  => ✅) produces ✅ always (true. D0)  for posterity. 
🎲2 🍎 => ✅  # produces ✅ with D2 odds, always consumes 🍎 regardless 
🍎 => ✅ 🎲2  # ...hmmmmmm produces randomness as a resource to use in subsequent recipes..?
# alternative:
🎲2 🍎 => 🎲3 ✅ # consumes 🍎 with D2 odds, produces ✅ with D3 odds... 
🍎 => 🎲3 ✅ # always consumes, D3 odds to produce
🎲2 🍎 => ✅ # always produces, D2 odds to consume
# alternative:
🍎 🎲4=> ✅ # does the entire recipe with D4 odds, or not at all
# alternative (we probably need ingredient-level granularity for this to work):
🍎2🎲4 🍌🎲3 => ✅🎲5 # consumes 🍎2 with D4 odds, consumes 🍌 with D3 odds (independent), produces ✅ with D5 odds (independent of costs)
# alternative: sometimes we want dependence.  e.g. probably caught a fish, but which one:
🎣 => 🐟%20 🐠%30 🐡%40  # last %10 is nothing.  no chance of 2 fish from one line
# alternative:
🎣 => 🎲2 🐟
🐟 => 2🎲4 🐠 # blegh I hate it no

# solution: (to the connections issue)
📏 => ➡ ⬅ 📏 # any width unlocks right-left path lol
📐 => ⬇ ⬆ 📐 # any height unlocks down-up path lol
❗2 📏2 📐2 ➡ ⬇ => 📏 📐 ➡(📏 📐) ⬇(📏 📐) # once connections established, siphon growth evenly
❗ 📐 ➡ => ➡(📐) # then siphon growth to the right
❗ 📏 ⬇ => ⬇(📏) # and down
# should deplete growth in both directions before they can try to re-attempt connection forming

# so now once again just the 2* interior cells doubled issue

❗3 📏x ⬅ => 📏2x  # how bout trade left connection for 2x if leftmost col? 
❗3 📐y ⬆ => 📐2y # or top row
# then the usual:
📏 => ➡ ⬅ 📏 # any width unlocks right-left path lol
📐 => ⬇ ⬆ 📐 # any height unlocks down-up path lol
❗2 📏2 📐2 ➡ ⬇ => 📏 📐 ➡(📏 📐) ⬇(📏 📐) # once connections established, siphon growth evenly
❗ 📐 ➡ => ➡(📐) # then siphon growth to the right
❗ 📏 ⬇ => ⬇(📏) # and down
# now everything should be 2x 2y in the end.  Good enough.

# then to reduce back down to 1x 1y:
❗2 📏2 📐2 ➡ ⬇ => ➡(📏 📐) ⬇(📏 📐) # always preserve quantity, instead

# problem: still want to be able to close the right-connection at end of row

# SMART ANSWER: (just do x in 1D, THEN do y for each x)
# ignore second dimension: JUST 1D X
📏2 => ➡ ⬅ 📏2 # any width>1 unlocks right-left path lol
📏 => ⬅ # else endpoint
❗2 📏x ⬅ => 📏x # if left col, cut left-connection (wall)

📐2 => ⬇ ⬆ 📐2 # any height>1 unlocks down-up path lol
📐 => ⬆ # else endpoint
❗2 📐y ⬆ => 📐y # if top row, cut up-connection (wall)
❗ 📐 ⬇ => ⬇(📐) # once connections established, siphon growth downwards

# 2-STEP reduced general observations: (only showing the new rules)
# note these dont preserve order.   they just say "if you have these two moves you can reach this with some combination"
⬜ 👉2 => 🔳 # OBSERVATION aka edge => edge
🔳' 👉2 => 🔳' 
⬜ 👉 ⤴ => ⏹
⬜ 👉 ↔ => 🔳
⬜ 👉 ↔ => ⬜  # PROBLEM: order matters!
⬜ 👉 ↔ => ⏹  # so it's actually more general
⬜ 👉 ⤵ => ⏹
🔳' 👉 ↔ => 🔳'
⬜ ⤴2 => ⏹
⬜ ⤴ ↔ => ⏹
⬜ ⤴ ⤵ => ⏹
⬜ ↔2 => ⬜
⬜ ↔ ⤵ => ⏹
⬜ ⤵ ↔ => ⏹
⬜ ⤵2 => ⏹

# SUMMARY (2-step) can discover a bunch of extra rules by brute force here
# LOOPS discoverable: (but would still take many more steps to confirm.... which is a very good strategy lol)
🔳' 👉 => 🔳' # edges are absorbing
⏹' ↔2 <=> ⏹' # turn around twice
⏹' ⤴ ⤵ <=> ⏹' # turns reset
# which can then be reduced to:
↔2 <=> # (this is a guess though, would need to be confirmed many times as it's possible the state is conditional)
⤴ ⤵ <=> # same
⤴2 <=> ↔ # note: loops aren't just to blanks, they're just two-way relationships between any states

# ...
# 3-LOOPS discoverable:
⤵2 ↔ <=>
⤴2 ↔ <=>
#### ⬜ 👉2 ↔ (⬜ 👉 ↔ (⬜ 👉 => ⏹) => ⬜)  # step, 180, step.  complex to represent this forced order hmmm
  # error not actually a loop anyway! cuz changes direction hah
🔳' 👉 ↔2 (🔳' 👉 ↔ (🔳' 👉 => 🔳') => ⏹) => 🔳' # absorbing wall and 2x reverse
# ... probably more but just testing out

⤵4 <=>
⤴4 <=>


# LOOP CHECKLIST (basically inferred by 4-step stage, if rules are checked for loopiness)
# rotates are reversible: (these are second-order rules though)
⤵ ⤴ <=> # nothing
↔2 <=> # nothing
⤵2 <=> ↔
⤵3 <=> ⤴
⤵4 <=>  # nothing
⤴2 <=> ↔
⤴3 <=> ⤵
⤴4 <=>  # nothing


# GENERAL TINKERING:  ABILITIES WE'VE SHOWN:
- we know how to represent multiple steps (in an ordered way) as inputs => outputs
  - A: use the inference formula as an input (bit of a stretch of syntax but it's probably the best idea we have)
- we can do automated 1-step rule discovery easily
  - A: just track input state and output state.  base observation ground truth
- we can do automated rule generalizing (given basic proposed objects)
  - A: e.g. if given ⏹ => ⬜ | ⬛ | 🟧 | 🟫 | 🟦 then we can modify other rules by swapping these out
  - a general rule is only valid if (and while) it matches ALL applicable base operations.  this check is doable via algorithm
- we can invalidate rules
  - A: keep checking against observations.  If a rule maps to another rule they must be right
- we can reduce rules
  - A: same as generalizing, we can remove parts of the rule (e.g. same on both sides) and see if it's ever invalidated by observations
- we can discover loops
  - A: similarly, we can guess a loop rule and keep checking it to increase confidence
- we can do automated 2+-step rule discovery
  - A: each additional step rule takes the previous one as an input, and the output is the next step
  - this gives us ordered steps, which can be reduced from base observations down to generalized rules and guesses

- then actually code it lol

# GENERAL TINKERING: TRANSITION RULES ARE TOKENS ===============
# a token can be a transition rule, because all tokens ARE transition rules
⤵ => (🟧 => ⬛) # + other options
    ⤵ => (⬜ => ⬛)
    ⤵ => (⬜ => 🟧)
    ...
🟧 <=> (🟧 => 🟧) # only rule (Identity)
# in this way, pretty much any token can be represented in the pathway of transitions from the 🎬 starting token
# and similarly, distance from the 🏁 finish token
# this is in line with a poset from category theory, where the system is just an ordered chains which are all connected by splits and merges between the start and finish
